					\item[BHS] \he{וְשַׁ֣בְתִּֽי אֲנִ֗י וָאֶרְאֶה֙ אֶת־כָּל־הָ֣עֲשֻׁקִ֔ים אֲשֶׁ֥ר נַעֲשִׂ֖ים תַּ֣חַת הַשָּׁ֑מֶשׁ וְהִנֵּ֣ה ׀ דִּמְעַ֣ת הָעֲשֻׁקִ֗ים וְאֵ֤ין לָהֶם֙ מְנַחֵ֔ם וּמִיַּ֤ד עֹֽשְׁקֵיהֶם֙ כֹּ֔חַ וְאֵ֥ין לָהֶ֖ם מְנַחֵֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Καὶ ἐπέστρεψα ἐγὼ καὶ εἶδον συμπάσας τὰς συκοφαντίας τὰς γινομένας ὑπὸ τὸν ἥλιον· ἰδοὺ δάκρυον τῶν συκοφαντουμένων, καὶ οὐκ ἔστιν αὐτοῖς παρακαλῶν, καὶ ἀπὸ χειρὸς συκοφαντούντων αὐτοὺς ἰσχύς, καὶ οὐκ ἔστιν αὐτοῖς παρακαλῶν.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Then I returned and saw all the oppressions that are done under the sun: and, behold, the tears of such as were oppressed, and they had no comforter; and on the side of their oppressors there was power; but they had no comforter.
					\item[NIV] Again I looked and saw all the oppression that was taking place under the sun: I saw the tears of the oppressed— and they have no comforter; power was on the side of their oppressors— and they have no comforter.
					\item[NRSV] Again I saw all the oppressions that are practiced under the sun. Look, the tears of the oppressed—with no one to comfort them! On the side of their oppressors there was power—with no one to comfort them.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:2} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-2
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-2}

			Wherefore I praised the dead which are already dead more than the living which are yet alive.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְשַׁבֵּ֧חַ אֲנִ֛י אֶת־הַמֵּתִ֖ים שֶׁכְּבָ֣ר מֵ֑תוּ מִן־הַ֣חַיִּ֔ים אֲשֶׁ֛ר הֵ֥מָּה חַיִּ֖ים עֲדֶֽנָה׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καὶ ἐπῄνεσα ἐγὼ σύμπαντας τοὺς τεθνηκότας τοὺς ἤδη ἀποθανόντας ὑπὲρ τοὺς ζῶντας, ὅσοι αὐτοὶ ζῶσιν ἕως τοῦ νῦν·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Wherefore I praised the dead that have been long dead more than the living that are yet alive;
					\item[NIV] And I declared that the dead, who had already died, are happier than the living, who are still alive.
					\item[NRSV] And I thought the dead, who have already died, more fortunate than the living, who are still alive;
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:3} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-3
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-3}

			Yea, better is he than both they, which hath not yet been, who hath not seen the evil work that is done under the sun.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְטוֹב֙ מִשְּׁנֵיהֶ֔ם אֵ֥ת אֲשֶׁר־עֲדֶ֖ן לֹ֣א הָיָ֑ה אֲשֶׁ֤ר לֹֽא־רָאָה֙ אֶת־הַמַּעֲשֶׂ֣ה הָרָ֔ע אֲשֶׁ֥ר נַעֲשָׂ֖ה תַּ֥חַת הַשָּֽׁמֶשׁ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καὶ ἀγαθὸς ὑπὲρ τοὺς δύο τούτους ὅστις οὔπω ἐγένετο, ὃς οὐκ εἶδεν σὺν πᾶν τὸ ποίημα τὸ πονηρὸν τὸ πεποιημένον ὑπὸ τὸν ἥλιον.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] yea, better than them both did I esteem him that hath not yet been, who hath not seen the evil work that is done under the sun.
					\item[NIV] But better than both is the one who has never been born, who has not seen the evil that is done under the sun.
					\item[NRSV] but better than both is the one who has not yet been, and has not seen the evil deeds that are done under the sun.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:4} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-4
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-4}

			Again, I considered all travail, and every right work, that for this a man is envied of his neighbour. This is also vanity and vexation of spirit.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְרָאִ֨יתִֽי אֲנִ֜י אֶת־כָּל־עָמָ֗ל וְאֵת֙ כָּל־כִּשְׁר֣וֹן הַֽמַּעֲשֶׂ֔ה כִּ֛י הִ֥יא קִנְאַת־אִ֖ישׁ מֵרֵעֵ֑הוּ גַּם־זֶ֥ה הֶ֖בֶל וּרְע֥וּת רֽוּחַ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Καὶ ἴδον ἐγὼ σύμπαντα τὸν μόχθον καὶ σύμπασαν ἀνδρείαν τοῦ ποιήματος, ὅτι τὸ ζῆλος ἀνδρὶ ἀπὸ τοῦ ἑταίρου αὐτοῦ· καί γε τοῦτο ματαιότης καὶ προαίρεσις πνεύματος.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Then I saw all labor and every skilful work, that for this a man is envied of his neighbor. This also is vanity and a striving after wind.
					\item[NIV] And I saw that all toil and all achievement spring from one person’s envy of another. This too is meaningless, a chasing after the wind.
					\item[NRSV] Then I saw that all toil and all skill in work come from one person’s envy of another. This also is vanity and a chasing after wind.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:5} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-5
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-5}

			The fool foldeth his hands together, and eateth his own flesh.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{הַכְּסִיל֙ חֹבֵ֣ק אֶת־יָדָ֔יו וְאֹכֵ֖ל אֶת־בְּשָׂרֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὁ ἄφρων περιέλαβεν τὰς χεῖρας αὐτοῦ καὶ ἔφαγεν τὰς σάρκας αὐτοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] The fool foldeth his hands together, and eateth his own flesh.
					\item[NIV] Fools fold their hands and ruin themselves.
					\item[NRSV] Fools fold their hands and consume their own flesh.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:6} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-6
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-6}

			Better is an handful with quietness, than both the hands full with travail and vexation of spirit.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֕וֹב מְלֹ֥א כַ֖ף נָ֑חַת מִמְּלֹ֥א חָפְנַ֛יִם עָמָ֖ל וּרְע֥וּת רֽוּחַ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὸν πλήρωμα δρακὸς ἀναπαύσεως ὑπὲρ πλήρωμα δύο δρακῶν μόχθου καὶ προαιρέσεως πνεύματος.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Better is a handful, with quietness, than two handfuls with labor and striving after wind.
					\item[NIV] Better one handful with tranquillity than two handfuls with toil and chasing after the wind.
					\item[NRSV] Better is a handful with quiet than two handfuls with toil, and a chasing after wind.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:7} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-7
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-7}

			Then I returned, and I saw vanity under the sun.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְשַׁ֧בְתִּי אֲנִ֛י וָאֶרְאֶ֥ה הֶ֖בֶל תַּ֥חַת הַשָּֽׁמֶשׁ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Καὶ ἐπέστρεψα ἐγὼ καὶ ἴδον ματαιότητα ὑπὸ τὸν ἥλιον.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Then I returned and saw vanity under the sun.
					\item[NIV] Again I saw something meaningless under the sun:
					\item[NRSV] Again, I saw vanity under the sun:
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:8} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-8
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-8}

			There is one alone, and there is not a second; yea, he hath neither child nor brother: yet is there no end of all his labour; neither is his eye satisfied with riches; neither saith he, For whom do I labour, and bereave my soul of good? This is also vanity, yea, it is a sore travail.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{יֵ֣שׁ אֶחָד֩ וְאֵ֨ין שֵׁנִ֜י גַּ֣ם בֵּ֧ן וָאָ֣ח אֵֽין־ל֗וֹ וְאֵ֥ין קֵץ֙ לְכָל־עֲמָל֔וֹ עֵינ֖וֹ לֹא־תִשְׂבַּ֣ע עֹ֑שֶׁר וּלְמִ֣י ׀ אֲנִ֣י עָמֵ֗ל וּמְחַסֵּ֤ר אֶת־נַפְשִׁי֙ מִטּוֹבָ֔ה גַּם־זֶ֥ה הֶ֛בֶל וְעִנְיַ֥ן רָ֖ע הֽוּא׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἔστιν εἷς καὶ οὐκ ἔστιν δεύτερος, καί γε υἱὸς καί γε ἀδελφὸς οὐκ ἔστιν αὐτῷ· καὶ οὐκ ἔστιν περασμὸς τῷ παντὶ μόχθῳ αὐτοῦ, καί γε ὀφθαλμὸς αὐτοῦ οὐκ ἐμπίπλαται πλούτου. καὶ τίνι ἐγὼ μοχθῶ καὶ στερίσκω τὴν ψυχήν μου ἀπὸ ἀγαθωσύνης; καί γε τοῦτο ματαιότης καὶ περισπασμὸς πονηρός ἐστιν.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] There is one that is alone, and he hath not a second; yea, he hath neither son nor brother; yet is there no end of all his labor, neither are his eyes satisfied with riches. For whom then, saith he, do I labor, and deprive my soul of good? This also is vanity, yea, it is a sore travail.
					\item[NIV] There was a man all alone; he had neither son nor brother. There was no end to his toil, yet his eyes were not content with his wealth. “For whom am I toiling,” he asked, “and why am I depriving myself of enjoyment?” This too is meaningless— a miserable business!
					\item[NRSV] the case of solitary individuals, without sons or brothers; yet there is no end to all their toil, and their eyes are never satisfied with riches. “For whom am I toiling,” they ask, “and depriving myself of pleasure?” This also is vanity and an unhappy business.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:9} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-9
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-9}

			Two are better than one; because they have a good reward for their labour.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{טוֹבִ֥ים הַשְּׁנַ֖יִם מִן־הָאֶחָ֑ד אֲשֶׁ֧ר יֵשׁ־לָהֶ֛ם שָׂכָ֥ר ט֖וֹב בַּעֲמָלָֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθοὶ δύο ὑπὲρ τὸν ἕνα, οἷς ἐστιν αὐτοῖς μισθὸς ἀγαθὸς ἐν μόχθῳ αὐτῶν·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Two are better than one, because they have a good reward for their labor.
					\item[NIV] Two are better than one, because they have a good return for their labor:
					\item[NRSV] Two are better than one, because they have a good reward for their toil.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:10} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-10
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-10}

			For if they fall, the one will lift up his fellow: but woe to him that is alone when he falleth; for he hath not another to help him up.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֣י אִם־יִפֹּ֔לוּ הָאֶחָ֖ד יָקִ֣ים אֶת־חֲבֵר֑וֹ וְאִ֣יל֗וֹ הָֽאֶחָד֙ שֶׁיִּפּ֔וֹל וְאֵ֥ין שֵׁנִ֖י לַהֲקִימֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι ἐὰν πέσωσιν, ὁ εἷς ἐγερεῖ τὸν μέτοχον αὐτοῦ· καὶ οὐαὶ αὐτῷ τῷ ἑνὶ ὅταν πέσῃ καὶ μὴ ᾖ δεύτερος ἐγεῖραι αὐτόν.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For if they fall, the one will lift up his fellow; but woe to him that is alone when he falleth, and hath not another to lift him up.
					\item[NIV] If either of them falls down, one can help the other up. But pity anyone who falls and has no one to help them up.
					\item[NRSV] For if they fall, one will lift up the other; but woe to one who is alone and falls and does not have another to help.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:11} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-11
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-11}

			Again, if two lie together, then they have heat: but how can one be warm alone?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{גַּ֛ם אִם־יִשְׁכְּב֥וּ שְׁנַ֖יִם וְחַ֣ם לָהֶ֑ם וּלְאֶחָ֖ד אֵ֥יךְ יֵחָֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καί γε ἂν κοιμηθῶσιν δύο, καὶ θέρμη αὐτοῖς· καὶ ὁ εἷς πῶς θερμανθῇ;}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Again, if two lie together, then they have warmth; but how can one be warm alone?
					\item[NIV] Also, if two lie down together, they will keep warm. But how can one keep warm alone?
					\item[NRSV] Again, if two lie together, they keep warm; but how can one keep warm alone?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:12} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-12
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-12}

			And if one prevail against him, two shall withstand him; and a threefold cord is not quickly broken.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְאִֽם־יִתְקְפוֹ֙ הָאֶחָ֔ד הַשְּׁנַ֖יִם יַעַמְד֣וּ נֶגְדּ֑וֹ וְהַחוּט֙ הַֽמְשֻׁלָּ֔שׁ לֹ֥א בִמְהֵרָ֖ה יִנָּתֵֽק׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καὶ ἐὰν ἐπικραταιωθῇ ὁ εἷς, οἱ δύο στήσονται κατέναντι αὐτοῦ, καὶ τὸ σπαρτίον τὸ ἔντριτον οὐ ταχὺ ἀπορραγήσεται.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] And if a man prevail against him that is alone, two shall withstand him; and a threefold cord is not quickly broken.
					\item[NIV] Though one may be overpowered, two can defend themselves. A cord of three strands is not quickly broken.
					\item[NRSV] And though one might prevail against another, two will withstand one. A threefold cord is not quickly broken.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:13} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-13
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-13}

			Better is a poor and a wise child than an old and foolish king, who will no more be admonished.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֛וֹב יֶ֥לֶד מִסְכֵּ֖ן וְחָכָ֑ם מִמֶּ֤לֶךְ זָקֵן֙ וּכְסִ֔יל אֲשֶׁ֛ר לֹא־יָדַ֥ע לְהִזָּהֵ֖ר עֽוֹד׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Ἀγαθὸς παῖς πένης καὶ σοφὸς ὑπὲρ βασιλέα πρεσβύτερον καὶ ἄφρονα, ὃς οὐκ ἔγνω τοῦ προσέχειν ἔτι·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Better is a poor and wise youth than an old and foolish king, who knoweth not how to receive admonition any more.
					\item[NIV] Better a poor but wise youth than an old but foolish king who no longer knows how to heed a warning.
					\item[NRSV] Better is a poor but wise youth than an old but foolish king, who will no longer take advice.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:14} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-14
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-14}

			For out of prison he cometh to reign; whereas also he that is born in his kingdom becometh poor.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּֽי־מִבֵּ֥ית הָסוּרִ֖ים יָצָ֣א לִמְלֹ֑ךְ כִּ֛י גַּ֥ם בְּמַלְכוּת֖וֹ נוֹלַ֥ד רָֽשׁ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι ἐξ οἴκου τῶν δεσμίων ἐξελεύσεται τοῦ βασιλεῦσαι, ὅτι καί γε ἐν βασιλείᾳ αὐτοῦ ἐγενήθη πένης.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For out of prison he came forth to be king; yea, even in his kingdom he was born poor.
					\item[NIV] The youth may have come from prison to the kingship, or he may have been born in poverty within his kingdom.
					\item[NRSV] One can indeed come out of prison to reign, even though born poor in the kingdom.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:15} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-15
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-15}

			I considered all the living which walk under the sun, with the second child that shall stand up in his stead.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{רָאִ֙יתִי֙ אֶת־כָּל־הַ֣חַיִּ֔ים הַֽמְהַלְּכִ֖ים תַּ֣חַת הַשָּׁ֑מֶשׁ עִ֚ם הַיֶּ֣לֶד הַשֵּׁנִ֔י אֲשֶׁ֥ר יַעֲמֹ֖ד תַּחְתָּֽיו׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἴδον σύμπαντας τοὺς ζῶντας τοὺς περιπατοῦντας ὑπὸ τὸν ἥλιον μετὰ τοῦ νεανίσκου τοῦ δευτέρου, ὃς στήσεται ἀντ᾽ αὐτοῦ·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] I saw all the living that walk under the sun, that they were with the youth, the second, that stood up in his stead.
					\item[NIV] I saw that all who lived and walked under the sun followed the youth, the king’s successor.
					\item[NRSV] I saw all the living who, moving about under the sun, follow that youth who replaced the king;
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 4:16} % *ECCLESIASTES-4-16
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-4-16}

			There is no end of all the people, even of all that have been before them: they also that come after shall not rejoice in him. Surely this also is vanity and vexation of spirit.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אֵֽין־קֵ֣ץ לְכָל־הָעָ֗ם לְכֹ֤ל אֲשֶׁר־הָיָה֙ לִפְנֵיהֶ֔ם גַּ֥ם הָאַחֲרוֹנִ֖ים לֹ֣א יִשְׂמְחוּ־ב֑וֹ כִּֽי־גַם־זֶ֥ה הֶ֖בֶל וְרַעְי֥וֹן רֽוּחַ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{οὐκ ἔστιν περασμὸς τῷ παντὶ λαῷ, τοῖς πᾶσιν οἳ ἐγένοντο ἔμπροσθεν αὐτῶν· καί γε οἱ ἔσχατοι οὐκ εὐφρανθήσονται ἐπ᾽ αὐτῷ. ὅτι καί γε τοῦτο ματαιότης καὶ προαίρεσις πνεύματος.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] There was no end of all the people, even of all them over whom he was: yet they that come after shall not rejoice in him. Surely this also is vanity and a striving after wind.
					\item[NIV] There was no end to all the people who were before them. But those who came later were not pleased with the successor. This too is meaningless, a chasing after the wind.
					\item[NRSV] there was no end to all those people whom he led. Yet those who come later will not rejoice in him. Surely this also is vanity and a chasing after wind.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}
	% -----
	\subsubsection{Ecclesiastes 5} % *ECCLESIASTES-5
	% -----
		\label{ECCLESIASTES-5}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:1} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-1
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-1}

			Keep thy foot when thou goest to the house of God, and be more ready to hear, than to give the sacrifice of fools: for they consider not that they do evil.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{שְׁמֹ֣ר רַגְלְךָ֗ כַּאֲשֶׁ֤ר תֵּלֵךְ֙ אֶל־בֵּ֣ית הָאֱלֹהִ֔ים וְקָר֣וֹב לִשְׁמֹ֔עַ מִתֵּ֥ת הַכְּסִילִ֖ים זָ֑בַח כִּֽי־אֵינָ֥ם יוֹדְעִ֖ים לַעֲשׂ֥וֹת רָֽע׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Φύλαξον τὸν πόδα σου ἐν ᾧ ἐὰν πορεύῃ εἰς οἶκον τοῦ θεοῦ, καὶ ἐγγὺς τοῦ ἀκούειν· ὑπὲρ δόμα τῶν ἀφρόνων θυσία σου, ὅτι οὐκ εἰσὶν εἰδότες τοῦ ποιεῖν κακόν.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Keep thy foot when thou goest to the house of God; for to draw nigh to hear is better than to give the sacrifice of fools: for they know not that they do evil.
					\item[NIV] Guard your steps when you go to the house of God. Go near to listen rather than to offer the sacrifice of fools, who do not know that they do wrong.
					\item[NRSV] Guard your steps when you go to the house of God; to draw near to listen is better than the sacrifice offered by fools; for they do not know how to keep from doing evil.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:2} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-2
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-2}

			Be not rash with thy mouth, and let not thine heart be hasty to utter any thing before God: for God is in heaven, and thou upon earth: therefore let thy words be few.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אַל־תְּבַהֵ֨ל עַל־פִּ֜יךָ וְלִבְּךָ֧ אַל־יְמַהֵ֛ר לְהוֹצִ֥יא דָבָ֖ר לִפְנֵ֣י הָאֱלֹהִ֑ים כִּ֣י הָאֱלֹהִ֤ים בַּשָּׁמַ֙יִם֙ וְאַתָּ֣ה עַל־הָאָ֔רֶץ עַֽל־כֵּ֛ן יִהְי֥וּ דְבָרֶ֖יךָ מְעַטִּֽים׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{μὴ σπεῦδε ἐπὶ στόματί σου, καὶ καρδία σου μὴ ταχυνάτω τοῦ ἐξενέγκαι λόγον πρὸ προσώπου τοῦ θεοῦ· ὅτι ὁ θεὸς ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ ἄνω, καὶ σὺ ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς· διὰ τοῦτο ἔστωσαν οἱ λόγοι σου ὀλίγοι.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Be not rash with thy mouth, and let not thy heart be hasty to utter anything before God; for God is in heaven, and thou upon earth: therefore let thy words be few.
					\item[NIV] Do not be quick with your mouth, do not be hasty in your heart to utter anything before God. God is in heaven and you are on earth, so let your words be few.
					\item[NRSV] Never be rash with your mouth, nor let your heart be quick to utter a word before God, for God is in heaven, and you upon earth; therefore let your words be few.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:3} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-3
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-3}

			For a dream cometh through the multitude of business; and a fool’s voice is known by multitude of words.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֛י בָּ֥א הַחֲל֖וֹם בְּרֹ֣ב עִנְיָ֑ן וְק֥וֹל כְּסִ֖יל בְּרֹ֥ב דְּבָרִֽים׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι παραγίνεται ἐνύπνιον ἐν πλήθει πειρασμοῦ, καὶ φωνὴ ἄφρονος ἐν πλήθει λόγων.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For a dream cometh with a multitude of business, and a fool's voice with a multitude of words.
					\item[NIV] A dream comes when there are many cares, and many words mark the speech of a fool.
					\item[NRSV] For dreams come with many cares, and a fool’s voice with many words.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:4} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-4
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-4}

			When thou vowest a vow unto God, defer not to pay it; for he hath no pleasure in fools: pay that which thou hast vowed.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כַּאֲשֶׁר֩ תִּדֹּ֨ר נֶ֜דֶר לֵֽאלֹהִ֗ים אַל־תְּאַחֵר֙ לְשַׁלְּמ֔וֹ כִּ֛י אֵ֥ין חֵ֖פֶץ בַּכְּסִילִ֑ים אֵ֥ת אֲשֶׁר־תִּדֹּ֖ר שַׁלֵּֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καθὼς εὔξῃ εὐχὴν τῷ θεῷ, μὴ χρονίσῃς τοῦ ἀποδοῦναι αὐτήν. οὐκ ἔστιν θέλημα ἐν ἄφροσιν· σὺ οὖν ὅσα ἐὰν εὔξῃ ἀπόδος.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] When thou vowest a vow unto God, defer not to pay it; for he hath no pleasure in fools: pay that which thou vowest.
					\item[NIV] When you make a vow to God, do not delay to fulfill it. He has no pleasure in fools; fulfill your vow.
					\item[NRSV] When you make a vow to God, do not delay fulfilling it; for he has no pleasure in fools. Fulfill what you vow.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:5} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-5
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-5}

			Better is it that thou shouldest not vow, than that thou shouldest vow and not pay.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֖וֹב אֲשֶׁ֣ר לֹֽא־תִדֹּ֑ר מִשֶׁתִּדּ֖וֹר וְלֹ֥א תְשַׁלֵּֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὸν τὸ μὴ εὔξασθαί σε, ἢ τὸ εὔξασθαί σε καὶ μὴ ἀποδοῦναι.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Better is it that thou shouldest not vow, than that thou shouldest vow and not pay.
					\item[NIV] It is better not to make a vow than to make one and not fulfill it.
					\item[NRSV] It is better that you should not vow than that you should vow and not fulfill it.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:6} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-6
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-6}

			Suffer not thy mouth to cause thy flesh to sin; neither say thou before the angel, that it was an error: wherefore should God be angry at thy voice, and destroy the work of thine hands?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אַל־תִּתֵּ֤ן אֶת־פִּ֙יךָ֙ לַחֲטִ֣יא אֶת־בְּשָׂרֶ֔ךָ וְאַל־תֹּאמַר֙ לִפְנֵ֣י הַמַּלְאָ֔ךְ כִּ֥י שְׁגָגָ֖ה הִ֑יא לָ֣מָּה יִקְצֹ֤ף הָֽאֱלֹהִים֙ עַל־קוֹלֶ֔ךָ וְחִבֵּ֖ל אֶת־מַעֲשֵׂ֥ה יָדֶֽיךָ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{μὴ δῷς τὸ στόμα σου τοῦ ἐξαμαρτῆσαι τὴν σάρκα σου, καὶ μὴ εἴπῃς πρὸ προσώπου τοῦ θεοῦ ὅτι Ἄγνοιά ἐστιν, ἵνα μὴ ὀργισθῇ ὁ θεὸς ἐπὶ φωνῇ σου καὶ διαφθείρῃ τὰ ποιήματα χειρῶν σῶν.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Suffer not thy mouth to cause thy flesh to sin; neither say thou before the angel, that it was an error: wherefore should God be angry at thy voice, and destroy the work of thy hands?
					\item[NIV] Do not let your mouth lead you into sin. And do not protest to the temple messenger, “My vow was a mistake.” Why should God be angry at what you say and destroy the work of your hands?
					\item[NRSV] Do not let your mouth lead you into sin, and do not say before the messenger that it was a mistake; why should God be angry at your words, and destroy the work of your hands?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:7} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-7
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-7}

			For in the multitude of dreams and many words there are also divers vanities: but fear thou God.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֣י בְרֹ֤ב חֲלֹמוֹת֙ וַהֲבָלִ֔ים וּדְבָרִ֖ים הַרְבֵּ֑ה כִּ֥י אֶת־הָאֱלֹהִ֖ים יְרָֽא׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι ἐν πλήθει ἐνυπνίων καὶ ματαιοτήτων καὶ λόγων πολλῶν· ὅτι σὺ τὸν θεὸν φοβοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For in the multitude of dreams there are vanities, and in many words: but fear thou God.
					\item[NIV] Much dreaming and many words are meaningless. Therefore fear God.
					\item[NRSV] With many dreams come vanities and a multitude of words; but fear God.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:8} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-8
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-8}

			If thou seest the oppression of the poor, and violent perverting of judgment and justice in a province, marvel not at the matter: for he that is higher than the highest regardeth; and there be higher than they.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אִם־עֹ֣שֶׁק רָ֠שׁ וְגֵ֨זֶל מִשְׁפָּ֤ט וָצֶ֙דֶק֙ תִּרְאֶ֣ה בַמְּדִינָ֔ה אַל־תִּתְמַ֖הּ עַל־הַחֵ֑פֶץ כִּ֣י גָבֹ֜הַּ מֵעַ֤ל גָּבֹ֙הַ֙ שֹׁמֵ֔ר וּגְבֹהִ֖ים עֲלֵיהֶֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Ἐὰν συκοφαντίαν πένητος καὶ ἁρπαγὴν κρίματος καὶ δικαιοσύνης ἴδῃς ἐν χώρᾳ, μὴ θαυμάσῃς ἐπὶ τῷ πράγματι· ὑψηλὸς ἐπάνω ὑψηλοῦ φυλάξαι, καὶ ὑψηλοὶ ἐπ᾽ αὐτούς·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] If thou seest the oppression of the poor, and the violent taking away of justice and righteousness in a province, marvel not at the matter: for one higher than the high regardeth; and there are higher than they.
					\item[NIV] If you see the poor oppressed in a district, and justice and rights denied, do not be surprised at such things; for one official is eyed by a higher one, and over them both are others higher still.
					\item[NRSV] If you see in a province the oppression of the poor and the violation of justice and right, do not be amazed at the matter; for the high official is watched by a higher, and there are yet higher ones over them.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:9} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-9
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-9}

			Moreover the profit of the earth is for all: the king himself is served by the field.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְיִתְר֥וֹן אֶ֖רֶץ בַּכֹּ֣ל ה֑וּא מֶ֥לֶךְ לְשָׂדֶ֖ה נֶעֱבָֽד׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καὶ περισσεία γῆς· ἐπὶ παντί ἐστι βασιλεὺς τοῦ ἀγροῦ εἰργασμένου.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Moreover the profit of the earth is for all: the king himself is served by the field.
					\item[NIV] The increase from the land is taken by all; the king himself profits from the fields.
					\item[NRSV] But all things considered, this is an advantage for a land: a king for a plowed field.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:10} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-10
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-10}

			He that loveth silver shall not be satisfied with silver; nor he that loveth abundance with increase: this is also vanity.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אֹהֵ֥ב כֶּ֙סֶף֙ לֹא־יִשְׂבַּ֣ע כֶּ֔סֶף וּמִֽי־אֹהֵ֥ב בֶּהָמ֖וֹן לֹ֣א תְבוּאָ֑ה גַּם־זֶ֖ה הָֽבֶל׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαπῶν ἀργύριον οὐ πλησθήσεται ἀργυρίου· καὶ τίς ἠγάπησεν ἐν πλήθει αὐτῶν γένημα; καί γε τοῦτο ματαιότης.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] He that loveth silver shall not be satisfied with silver; nor he that loveth abundance, with increase: this also is vanity.
					\item[NIV] Whoever loves money never has enough; whoever loves wealth is never satisfied with their income. This too is meaningless.
					\item[NRSV] The lover of money will not be satisfied with money; nor the lover of wealth, with gain. This also is vanity.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:11} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-11
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-11}

			When goods increase, they are increased that eat them: and what good is there to the owners thereof, saving the beholding of them with their eyes?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{בִּרְבוֹת֙ הַטּוֹבָ֔ה רַבּ֖וּ אוֹכְלֶ֑יהָ וּמַה־כִּשְׁרוֹן֙ לִבְעָלֶ֔יהָ כִּ֖י רְא֥וּת עֵינָֽיו׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἐν πλήθει ἀγαθωσύνης ἐπληθύνθησαν ἔσθοντες αὐτήν· καὶ τί ἀνδρεία τῷ παρ᾽ αὐτῆς; ὅτι ἀρχὴ τοῦ ὁρᾷν ὀφθαλμὸν αὐτοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] When goods increase, they are increased that eat them; and what advantage is there to the owner thereof, save the beholding of them with his eyes?
					\item[NIV] As goods increase, so do those who consume them. And what benefit are they to the owners except to feast their eyes on them?
					\item[NRSV] When goods increase, those who eat them increase; and what gain has their owner but to see them with his eyes?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:12} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-12
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-12}

			The sleep of a labouring man is sweet, whether he eat little or much: but the abundance of the rich will not suffer him to sleep.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{מְתוּקָה֙ שְׁנַ֣ת הָעֹבֵ֔ד אִם־מְעַ֥ט וְאִם־הַרְבֵּ֖ה יֹאכֵ֑ל וְהַשָּׂבָע֙ לֶֽעָשִׁ֔יר אֵינֶ֛נּוּ מַנִּ֥יחַֽ ל֖וֹ לִישֽׁוֹן׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{γλυκὺς ὕπνος τοῦ δούλου εἰ ὀλίγον καὶ εἰ πολὺ φάγεται, καὶ τῷ ἐμπλησθέντι τοῦ πλουτῆσαι οὐκ ἔστιν ἀφίων αὐτὸν τοῦ ὑπνῶσαι.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] The sleep of a laboring man is sweet, whether he eat little or much; but the fulness of the rich will not suffer him to sleep.
					\item[NIV] The sleep of a laborer is sweet, whether they eat little or much, but as for the rich, their abundance permits them no sleep.
					\item[NRSV] Sweet is the sleep of laborers, whether they eat little or much; but the surfeit of the rich will not let them sleep.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:13} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-13
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-13}

			There is a sore evil which I have seen under the sun, namely, riches kept for the owners thereof to their hurt.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{יֵ֚שׁ רָעָ֣ה חוֹלָ֔ה רָאִ֖יתִי תַּ֣חַת הַשָּׁ֑מֶשׁ עֹ֛שֶׁר שָׁמ֥וּר לִבְעָלָ֖יו לְרָעָתֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἔστιν ἀρρωστία ἣν ἴδον ὑπὸ τὸν ἥλιον, πλοῦτον φυλασσόμενον τῷ παρ᾽ αὐτοῦ εἰς κακίαν αὐτῷ,}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] There is a grievous evil which I have seen under the sun, namely, riches kept by the owner thereof to his hurt:
					\item[NIV] I have seen a grievous evil under the sun: wealth hoarded to the harm of its owners,
					\item[NRSV] There is a grievous ill that I have seen under the sun: riches were kept by their owners to their hurt,
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:14} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-14
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-14}

			But those riches perish by evil travail: and he begetteth a son, and there is nothing in his hand.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְאָבַ֛ד הָעֹ֥שֶׁר הַה֖וּא בְּעִנְיַ֣ן רָ֑ע וְהוֹלִ֣יד בֵּ֔ן וְאֵ֥ין בְּיָד֖וֹ מְאֽוּמָה׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καὶ ἀπολεῖται ὁ πλοῦτος ἐκεῖνος ἐν περισπασμῷ πονηρῷ, καὶ ἐγέννησεν υἱόν, καὶ οὐκ ἔστιν ἐν χειρὶ αὐτοῦ οὐδέν·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] and those riches perish by evil adventure; and if he hath begotten a son, there is nothing in his hand.
					\item[NIV] or wealth lost through some misfortune, so that when they have children there is nothing left for them to inherit.
					\item[NRSV] and those riches were lost in a bad venture; though they are parents of children, they have nothing in their hands.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:15} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-15
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-15}

			As he came forth of his mother’s womb, naked shall he return to go as he came, and shall take nothing of his labour, which he may carry away in his hand.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כַּאֲשֶׁ֤ר יָצָא֙ מִבֶּ֣טֶן אִמּ֔וֹ עָר֛וֹם יָשׁ֥וּב לָלֶ֖כֶת כְּשֶׁבָּ֑א וּמְא֙וּמָה֙ לֹא־יִשָּׂ֣א בַעֲמָל֔וֹ שֶׁיֹּלֵ֖ךְ בְּיָדֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καθὼς ἐξῆλθεν ἀπὸ γαστρὸς μητρὸς αὐτοῦ γυμνός, ἐπιστρέψει τοῦ πορευθῆναι ὡς ἥκει, καὶ οὐδὲν οὐ λήμψεται ἐν μόχθῳ αὐτοῦ, ἵνα πορευθῇ ἐν χειρὶ αὐτοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] As he came forth from his mother's womb, naked shall he go again as he came, and shall take nothing for his labor, which he may carry away in his hand.
					\item[NIV] Everyone comes naked from their mother’s womb, and as everyone comes, so they depart. They take nothing from their toil that they can carry in their hands.
					\item[NRSV] As they came from their mother’s womb, so they shall go again, naked as they came; they shall take nothing for their toil, which they may carry away with their hands.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:16} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-16
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-16}

			And this also is a sore evil, that in all points as he came, so shall he go: and what profit hath he that hath laboured for the wind?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְגַם־זֹה֙ רָעָ֣ה חוֹלָ֔ה כָּל־עֻמַּ֥ת שֶׁבָּ֖א כֵּ֣ן יֵלֵ֑ךְ וּמַה־יִּתְר֣וֹן ל֔וֹ שֶֽׁיַּעֲמֹ֖ל לָרֽוּחַ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καί γε τοῦτο πονηρὰ ἀρρωστία· ὥσπερ γὰρ παρεγένετο, οὕτως καὶ ἀπελεύσεται· καὶ ἡ περισσεία αὐτῷ ᾗ μοχθεῖ εἰς ἄνεμον.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] And this also is a grievous evil, that in all points as he came, so shall he go: and what profit hath he that he laboreth for the wind?
					\item[NIV] This too is a grievous evil: As everyone comes, so they depart, and what do they gain, since they toil for the wind?
					\item[NRSV] This also is a grievous ill: just as they came, so shall they go; and what gain do they have from toiling for the wind?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:17} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-17
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-17}

			All his days also he eateth in darkness, and he hath much sorrow and wrath with his sickness.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{גַּ֥ם כָּל־יָמָ֖יו בַּחֹ֣שֶׁךְ יֹאכֵ֑ל וְכָעַ֥ס הַרְבֵּ֖ה וְחָלְי֥וֹ וָקָֽצֶף׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καί γε πᾶσαι αἱ ἡμέραι αὐτοῦ ἐν σκότει καὶ ἐν πένθει καὶ θυμῷ πολλῷ καὶ ἀρρωστίᾳ καὶ χόλῳ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] All his days also he eateth in darkness, and he is sore vexed, and hath sickness and wrath.
					\item[NIV] All their days they eat in darkness, with great frustration, affliction and anger.
					\item[NRSV] Besides, all their days they eat in darkness, in much vexation and sickness and resentment.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:18} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-18
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-18}

			Behold that which I have seen: it is good and comely for one to eat and to drink, and to enjoy the good of all his labour that he taketh under the sun all the days of his life, which God giveth him: for it is his portion.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{הִנֵּ֞ה אֲשֶׁר־רָאִ֣יתִי אָ֗נִי ט֣וֹב אֲשֶׁר־יָפֶ֣ה לֶֽאֶכוֹל־וְ֠לִשְׁתּוֹת וְלִרְא֨וֹת טוֹבָ֜ה בְּכָל־עֲמָל֣וֹ ׀ שֶׁיַּעֲמֹ֣ל תַּֽחַת־הַשֶּׁ֗מֶשׁ מִסְפַּ֧ר חַיָּ֛יו אֲשֶׁר־נָֽתַן־ל֥וֹ הָאֱלֹהִ֖ים כִּי־ה֥וּא חֶלְקֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Ἰδοὺ ἴδον ἐγὼ ἀγαθὸν ὅ ἐστιν καλόν, τοῦ φαγεῖν καὶ τοῦ πιεῖν καὶ τοῦ ἰδεῖν ἀγαθωσύνην ἐν παντὶ μόχθῳ αὐτοῦ ᾧ ἐὰν μοχθῇ ὑπὸ τὸν ἥλιον ἀριθμὸν ἡμερῶν ζωῆς αὐτοῦ ὧν ἔδωκεν αὐτῷ ὁ θεός· ὅτι αὐτὸ μερὶς αὐτοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Behold, that which I have seen to be good and to be comely is for one to eat and to drink, and to enjoy good in all his labor, wherein he laboreth under the sun, all the days of his life which God hath given him: for this is his portion.
					\item[NIV] This is what I have observed to be good: that it is appropriate for a person to eat, to drink and to find satisfaction in their toilsome labor under the sun during the few days of life God has given them—for this is their lot.
					\item[NRSV] This is what I have seen to be good: it is fitting to eat and drink and find enjoyment in all the toil with which one toils under the sun the few days of the life God gives us; for this is our lot.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:19} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-19
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-19}

			Every man also to whom God hath given riches and wealth, and hath given him power to eat thereof, and to take his portion, and to rejoice in his labour; this is the gift of God.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{גַּ֣ם כָּֽל־הָאָדָ֡ם אֲשֶׁ֣ר נָֽתַן־ל֣וֹ הָאֱלֹהִים֩ עֹ֨שֶׁר וּנְכָסִ֜ים וְהִשְׁלִיט֨וֹ לֶאֱכֹ֤ל מִמֶּ֙נּוּ֙ וְלָשֵׂ֣את אֶת־חֶלְק֔וֹ וְלִשְׂמֹ֖חַ בַּעֲמָל֑וֹ זֹ֕ה מַתַּ֥ת אֱלֹהִ֖ים הִֽיא׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καί γε πᾶς ἄνθρωπος ᾧ ἔδωκεν αὐτῷ ὁ θεὸς πλοῦτον καὶ ὑπάρχοντα, καὶ ἐξουσίασεν αὐτῶν, φαγεῖν ἀπ᾽ αὐτοῦ καὶ λαβεῖν τὸ μέρος αὐτοῦ καὶ τοῦ εὐφρανθῆναι ἐν μόχθῳ αὐτοῦ, τοῦτο δόμα θεοῦ ἐστίν.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Every man also to whom God hath given riches and wealth, and hath given him power to eat thereof, and to take his portion, and to rejoice in his labor — this is the gift of God.
					\item[NIV] Moreover, when God gives someone wealth and possessions, and the ability to enjoy them, to accept their lot and be happy in their toil—this is a gift of God.
					\item[NRSV] Likewise all to whom God gives wealth and possessions and whom he enables to enjoy them, and to accept their lot and find enjoyment in their toil—this is the gift of God.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 5:20} % *ECCLESIASTES-5-20
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-5-20}

			For he shall not much remember the days of his life; because God answereth him in the joy of his heart.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֚י לֹ֣א הַרְבֵּ֔ה יִזְכֹּ֖ר אֶת־יְמֵ֣י חַיָּ֑יו כִּ֧י הָאֱלֹהִ֛ים מַעֲנֶ֖ה בְּשִׂמְחַ֥ת לִבּֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι οὐκ ἄλλα μνησθήσεται τὰς ἡμέρας τῆς ζωῆς αὐτοῦ· ὅτι ὁ θεὸς περισπᾷ αὐτὸν ἐν εὐφροσύνῃ καρδίας αὐτοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For he shall not much remember the days of his life; because God answereth him in the joy of his heart.
					\item[NIV] They seldom reflect on the days of their life, because God keeps them occupied with gladness of heart.
					\item[NRSV] For they will scarcely brood over the days of their lives, because God keeps them occupied with the joy of their hearts.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}
	% -----
	\subsubsection{Ecclesiastes 6} % *ECCLESIASTES-6
	% -----
		\label{ECCLESIASTES-6}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:1} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-1
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-1}

			There is an evil which I have seen under the sun, and it is common among men:

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{יֵ֣שׁ רָעָ֔ה אֲשֶׁ֥ר רָאִ֖יתִי תַּ֣חַת הַשָּׁ֑מֶשׁ וְרַבָּ֥ה הִ֖יא עַל־הָאָדָֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Ἔστιν πονηρία ἣν ἴδον ὑπὸ τὸν ἥλιον, καὶ πολλή ἐστιν ὑπὸ τὸν ἄνθρωπον·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] There is an evil which I have seen under the sun, and it is heavy upon men:
					\item[NIV] I have seen another evil under the sun, and it weighs heavily on mankind:
					\item[NRSV] There is an evil that I have seen under the sun, and it lies heavy upon humankind:
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:2} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-2
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-2}

			A man to whom God hath given riches, wealth, and honour, so that he wanteth nothing for his soul of all that he desireth, yet God giveth him not power to eat thereof, but a stranger eateth it: this is vanity, and it is an evil disease.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אִ֣ישׁ אֲשֶׁ֣ר יִתֶּן־ל֣וֹ הָאֱלֹהִ֡ים עֹשֶׁר֩ וּנְכָסִ֨ים וְכָב֜וֹד וְֽאֵינֶ֨נּוּ חָסֵ֥ר לְנַפְשׁ֣וֹ ׀ מִכֹּ֣ל אֲשֶׁר־יִתְאַוֶּ֗ה וְלֹֽא־יַשְׁלִיטֶ֤נּוּ הָֽאֱלֹהִים֙ לֶאֱכֹ֣ל מִמֶּ֔נּוּ כִּ֛י אִ֥ישׁ נָכְרִ֖י יֹֽאכֲלֶ֑נּוּ זֶ֥ה הֶ֛בֶל וָחֳלִ֥י רָ֖ע הֽוּא׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀνὴρ ᾧ δώσει αὐτῷ ὁ θεὸς πλοῦτον καὶ ὑπάρχοντα καὶ δόξαν, καὶ οὐκ ἔστιν ὑστερῶν τῇ ψυχῇ αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ πάντων ὧν ἐπιθυμήσει· καὶ οὐκ ἐξουσιάσει αὐτῷ ὁ θεὸς τοῦ φαγεῖν ἀπ᾽ αὐτοῦ, ὅτι ἀνὴρ ξένος φάγεται αὐτόν. τοῦτο ματαιότης καὶ ἀρρωστία πονηρά ἐστιν.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] a man to whom God giveth riches, wealth, and honor, so that he lacketh nothing for his soul of all that he desireth, yet God giveth him not power to eat thereof, but an alien eateth it; this is vanity, and it is an evil disease.
					\item[NIV] God gives some people wealth, possessions and honor, so that they lack nothing their hearts desire, but God does not grant them the ability to enjoy them, and strangers enjoy them instead. This is meaningless, a grievous evil.
					\item[NRSV] those to whom God gives wealth, possessions, and honor, so that they lack nothing of all that they desire, yet God does not enable them to enjoy these things, but a stranger enjoys them. This is vanity; it is a grievous ill.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:3} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-3
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-3}

			If a man beget an hundred children, and live many years, so that the days of his years be many, and his soul be not filled with good, and also that he have no burial; I say, that an untimely birth is better than he.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אִם־יוֹלִ֣יד אִ֣ישׁ מֵאָ֡ה וְשָׁנִים֩ רַבּ֨וֹת יִֽחְיֶ֜ה וְרַ֣ב ׀ שֶׁיִּהְי֣וּ יְמֵֽי־שָׁנָ֗יו וְנַפְשׁוֹ֙ לֹא־תִשְׂבַּ֣ע מִן־הַטּוֹבָ֔ה וְגַם־קְבוּרָ֖ה לֹא־הָ֣יְתָה לּ֑וֹ אָמַ֕רְתִּי ט֥וֹב מִמֶּ֖נּוּ הַנָּֽפֶל׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἐὰν γεννήσῃ ἀνὴρ ἑκατόν, καὶ ἔτη πολλὰ ζήσεται, καὶ πλῆθος ὅ τι ἔσονται ἡμέραι ἐτῶν αὐτοῦ, καὶ ψυχὴ αὐτοῦ οὐ πλησθήσεται ἀπὸ τῆς ἀγαθωσύνης, καί γε ταφὴ οὐκ ἐγένετο αὐτῷ, εἶπα Ἀγαθὸν ὑπὲρ αὐτὸν τὸ ἔκτρωμα·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] If a man beget a hundred children, and live many years, so that the days of his years are many, but his soul be not filled with good, and moreover he have no burial; I say, that an untimely birth is better than he:
					\item[NIV] A man may have a hundred children and live many years; yet no matter how long he lives, if he cannot enjoy his prosperity and does not receive proper burial, I say that a stillborn child is better off than he.
					\item[NRSV] A man may beget a hundred children, and live many years; but however many are the days of his years, if he does not enjoy life’s good things, or has no burial, I say that a stillborn child is better off than he.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:4} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-4
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-4}

			For he cometh in with vanity, and departeth in darkness, and his name shall be covered with darkness.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּֽי־בַהֶ֥בֶל בָּ֖א וּבַחֹ֣שֶׁךְ יֵלֵ֑ךְ וּבַחֹ֖שֶׁךְ שְׁמ֥וֹ יְכֻסֶּֽה׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι ἐν ματαιότητι ἦλθεν καὶ ἐν σκότει πορεύεται, καὶ ἐν σκότει ὄνομα αὐτοῦ καλυφθήσεται,}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] for it cometh in vanity, and departeth in darkness, and the name thereof is covered with darkness;
					\item[NIV] It comes without meaning, it departs in darkness, and in darkness its name is shrouded.
					\item[NRSV] For it comes into vanity and goes into darkness, and in darkness its name is covered;
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:5} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-5
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-5}

			Moreover he hath not seen the sun, nor known any thing: this hath more rest than the other.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{גַּם־שֶׁ֥מֶשׁ לֹא־רָאָ֖ה וְלֹ֣א יָדָ֑ע נַ֥חַת לָזֶ֖ה מִזֶּֽה׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καί γε ἥλιον οὐκ εἶδεν, καὶ οὐκ ἔγνω ἀναπαύσεις τούτῳ ὑπὲρ τοῦτον.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] moreover it hath not seen the sun nor known it; this hath rest rather than the other:
					\item[NIV] Though it never saw the sun or knew anything, it has more rest than does that man—
					\item[NRSV] moreover it has not seen the sun or known anything; yet it finds rest rather than he.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:6} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-6
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-6}

			Yea, though he live a thousand years twice told, yet hath he seen no good: do not all go to one place?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{וְאִלּ֣וּ חָיָ֗ה אֶ֤לֶף שָׁנִים֙ פַּעֲמַ֔יִם וְטוֹבָ֖ה לֹ֣א רָאָ֑ה הֲלֹ֛א אֶל־מָק֥וֹם אֶחָ֖ד הַכֹּ֥ל הוֹלֵֽךְ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καὶ εἰ ἔζησεν χιλίων ἐτῶν καθόδους, καὶ ἀγαθωσύνην οὐκ εἶδεν, μὴ οὐκ εἰς τόπον ἕνα πορεύεται;}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] yea, though he live a thousand years twice told, and yet enjoy no good, do not all go to one place?
					\item[NIV] even if he lives a thousand years twice over but fails to enjoy his prosperity. Do not all go to the same place?
					\item[NRSV] Even though he should live a thousand years twice over, yet enjoy no good—do not all go to one place?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:7} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-7
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-7}

			All the labour of man is for his mouth, and yet the appetite is not filled.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כָּל־עֲמַ֥ל הָאָדָ֖ם לְפִ֑יהוּ וְגַם־הַנֶּ֖פֶשׁ לֹ֥א תִמָּלֵֽא׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{πᾶς μόχθος ἀνθρώπου εἰς στόμα αὐτοῦ, καί γε ἡ ψυχὴ οὐ πληρωθήσεται.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] All the labor of man is for his mouth, and yet the appetite is not filled.
					\item[NIV] Everyone’s toil is for their mouth, yet their appetite is never satisfied.
					\item[NRSV] All human toil is for the mouth, yet the appetite is not satisfied.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:8} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-8
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-8}

			For what hath the wise more than the fool? what hath the poor, that knoweth to walk before the living?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֛י מַה־יּוֹתֵ֥ר לֶחָכָ֖ם מִֽן־הַכְּסִ֑יל מַה־לֶּעָנִ֣י יוֹדֵ֔עַ לַהֲלֹ֖ךְ נֶ֥גֶד הַחַיִּֽים׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι περισσεία τῷ σοφῷ ὑπὲρ τὸν ἄφρονα, διότι ὁ πένης οἶδεν πορευθῆναι κατέναντι τῆς ζωῆς.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For what advantage hath the wise more than the fool? or what hath the poor man, that knoweth how to walk before the living?
					\item[NIV] What advantage have the wise over fools? What do the poor gain by knowing how to conduct themselves before others?
					\item[NRSV] For what advantage have the wise over fools? And what do the poor have who know how to conduct themselves before the living?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:9} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-9
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-9}

			Better is the sight of the eyes than the wandering of the desire: this is also vanity and vexation of spirit.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֛וֹב מַרְאֵ֥ה עֵינַ֖יִם מֵֽהֲלָךְ־נָ֑פֶשׁ גַּם־זֶ֥ה הֶ֖בֶל וּרְע֥וּת רֽוּחַ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὸν ὅραμα ὀφθαλμῶν ὑπὲρ πορευόμενον ψυχῇ· καί γε τοῦτο ματαιότης καὶ προαίρεσις πνεύματος.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Better is the sight of the eyes than the wandering of the desire: this also is vanity and a striving after wind.
					\item[NIV] Better what the eye sees than the roving of the appetite. This too is meaningless, a chasing after the wind.
					\item[NRSV] Better is the sight of the eyes than the wandering of desire; this also is vanity and a chasing after wind.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:10} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-10
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-10}

			That which hath been is named already, and it is known that it is man: neither may he contend with him that is mightier than he.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{מַה־שֶּֽׁהָיָ֗ה כְּבָר֙ נִקְרָ֣א שְׁמ֔וֹ וְנוֹדָ֖ע אֲשֶׁר־ה֣וּא אָדָ֑ם וְלֹא־יוּכַ֣ל לָדִ֔ין עִ֥ם שֶׁתַּקִּ֖יף מִמֶּֽנּוּ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Εἴ τι ἐγένετο, ἤδη κέκληται ὄνομα αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἐγνώσθη ὅ ἐστιν ἄνθρωπος, καὶ οὐ δυνήσεται κριθῆναι μετὰ τοῦ ἰσχυροῦ ὑπὲρ αὐτόν·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Whatsoever hath been, the name thereof was given long ago; and it is known what man is; neither can he contend with him that is mightier than he.
					\item[NIV] Whatever exists has already been named, and what humanity is has been known; no one can contend with someone who is stronger.
					\item[NRSV] Whatever has come to be has already been named, and it is known what human beings are, and that they are not able to dispute with those who are stronger.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:11} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-11
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-11}

			Seeing there be many things that increase vanity, what is man the better?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֛י יֵשׁ־דְּבָרִ֥ים הַרְבֵּ֖ה מַרְבִּ֣ים הָ֑בֶל מַה־יֹּתֵ֖ר לָאָדָֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι εἰσὶν λόγοι πολλοὶ πληθύνοντες ματαιότητα.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Seeing there are many things that increase vanity, what is man the better?
					\item[NIV] The more the words, the less the meaning, and how does that profit anyone?
					\item[NRSV] The more words, the more vanity, so how is one the better?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 6:12} % *ECCLESIASTES-6-12
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-6-12}

			For who knoweth what is good for man in this life, all the days of his vain life which he spendeth as a shadow? for who can tell a man what shall be after him under the sun?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֣י מִֽי־יוֹדֵעַ֩ מַה־טּ֨וֹב לָֽאָדָ֜ם בַּֽחַיִּ֗ים מִסְפַּ֛ר יְמֵי־חַיֵּ֥י הֶבְל֖וֹ וְיַעֲשֵׂ֣ם כַּצֵּ֑ל אֲשֶׁר֙ מִֽי־יַגִּ֣יד לָֽאָדָ֔ם מַה־יִּהְיֶ֥ה אַחֲרָ֖יו תַּ֥חַת הַשָּֽׁמֶשׁ׃}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For who knoweth what is good for man in his life, all the days of his vain life which he spendeth as a shadow? for who can tell a man what shall be after him under the sun?
					\item[NIV] For who knows what is good for a person in life, during the few and meaningless days they pass through like a shadow? Who can tell them what will happen under the sun after they are gone?
					\item[NRSV] For who knows what is good for mortals while they live the few days of their vain life, which they pass like a shadow? For who can tell them what will be after them under the sun?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}
	% -----
	\subsubsection{Ecclesiastes 7} % *ECCLESIASTES-7
	% -----
		\label{ECCLESIASTES-7}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:1} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-1
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-1}

			A good name is better than precious ointment; and the day of death than the day of one’s birth.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֥וֹב שֵׁ֖ם מִשֶּׁ֣מֶן ט֑וֹב וְי֣וֹם הַמָּ֔וֶת מִיּ֖וֹם הִוָּלְדֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{τί περισσὸν τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ; ὅτι τίς οἶδεν ἀγαθὸν τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ ἐν τῇ ζωῇ, ἀριθμὸν ἡμερῶν ζωῆς ματαιότητος αὐτοῦ; καὶ ἐποίησεν αὐτὰ ἐν σκιᾷ· ὅτι τίς ἀπαγγελεῖ τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ τί ἔσται ὀπίσω αὐτοῦ ὑπὸ τὸν ἥλιον;}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] A good name is better than precious oil; and the day of death, than the day of one's birth.
					\item[NIV] A good name is better than fine perfume, and the day of death better than the day of birth.
					\item[NRSV] A good name is better than precious ointment, and the day of death, than the day of birth.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:2} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-2
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-2}

			It is better to go to the house of mourning, than to go to the house of feasting: for that is the end of all men; and the living will lay it to his heart.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֞וֹב לָלֶ֣כֶת אֶל־בֵּֽית־אֵ֗בֶל מִלֶּ֙כֶת֙ אֶל־בֵּ֣ית מִשְׁתֶּ֔ה בַּאֲשֶׁ֕ר ה֖וּא ס֣וֹף כָּל־הָאָדָ֑ם וְהַחַ֖י יִתֵּ֥ן אֶל־לִבּֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Ἀγαθὸν ὄνομα ὑπὲρ ἔλαιον ἀγαθόν, καὶ ἡμέρα τοῦ θανάτου ὑπὲρ ἡμέραν γεννήσεως.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] It is better to go to the house of mourning than to go to the house of feasting: for that is the end of all men; and the living will lay it to his heart.
					\item[NIV] It is better to go to a house of mourning than to go to a house of feasting, for death is the destiny of everyone; the living should take this to heart.
					\item[NRSV] It is better to go to the house of mourning than to go to the house of feasting; for this is the end of everyone, and the living will lay it to heart.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:3} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-3
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-3}

			Sorrow is better than laughter: for by the sadness of the countenance the heart is made better.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֥וֹב כַּ֖עַס מִשְּׂחֹ֑ק כִּֽי־בְרֹ֥עַ פָּנִ֖ים יִ֥יטַב לֵֽב׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὸν πορευθῆναι εἰς οἶκον πένθους ἢ ὅτι πορευθῆναι εἰς οἶκον πότου, καθότι τοῦτο τέλος παντὸς ἀνθρώπου, καὶ ὁ ζῶν δώσει ἀγαθὸν εἰς καρδίαν αὐτοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Sorrow is better than laughter; for by the sadness of the countenance the heart is made glad.
					\item[NIV] Frustration is better than laughter, because a sad face is good for the heart.
					\item[NRSV] Sorrow is better than laughter, for by sadness of countenance the heart is made glad.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:4} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-4
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-4}

			The heart of the wise is in the house of mourning; but the heart of fools is in the house of mirth.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{לֵ֤ב חֲכָמִים֙ בְּבֵ֣ית אֵ֔בֶל וְלֵ֥ב כְּסִילִ֖ים בְּבֵ֥ית שִׂמְחָֽה׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὸν θυμὸς ὑπὲρ γέλωτα, ὅτι ἐν κακίᾳ προσώπου ἀγαθυνθήσεται·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] The heart of the wise is in the house of mourning; but the heart of fools is in the house of mirth.
					\item[NIV] The heart of the wise is in the house of mourning, but the heart of fools is in the house of pleasure.
					\item[NRSV] The heart of the wise is in the house of mourning; but the heart of fools is in the house of mirth.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:5} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-5
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-5}

			It is better to hear the rebuke of the wise, than for a man to hear the song of fools.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֕וֹב לִשְׁמֹ֖עַ גַּעֲרַ֣ת חָכָ֑ם מֵאִ֕ישׁ שֹׁמֵ֖עַ שִׁ֥יר כְּסִילִֽים׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καρδία σοφῶν ἐν οἴκῳ πένθους, καὶ καρδία ἀφρόνων ἐν οἴκῳ εὐφροσύνης.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] It is better to hear the rebuke of the wise, than for a man to hear the song of fools.
					\item[NIV] It is better to heed the rebuke of a wise person than to listen to the song of fools.
					\item[NRSV] It is better to hear the rebuke of the wise than to hear the song of fools.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:6} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-6
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-6}

			For as the crackling of thorns under a pot, so is the laughter of the fool: this also is vanity.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֣י כְק֤וֹל הַסִּירִים֙ תַּ֣חַת הַסִּ֔יר כֵּ֖ן שְׂחֹ֣ק הַכְּסִ֑יל וְגַם־זֶ֖ה הָֽבֶל׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἀκοῦσαι ἐπιτίμησιν σοφοῦ ὑπὲρ ἄνδρα ἀκούοντα ᾆσμα ἀφρόνων.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For as the crackling of thorns under a pot, so is the laughter of the fool: this also is vanity.
					\item[NIV] Like the crackling of thorns under the pot, so is the laughter of fools. This too is meaningless.
					\item[NRSV] For like the crackling of thorns under a pot, so is the laughter of fools; this also is vanity.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:7} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-7
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-7}

			Surely oppression maketh a wise man mad; and a gift destroyeth the heart.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֥י הָעֹ֖שֶׁק יְהוֹלֵ֣ל חָכָ֑ם וִֽיאַבֵּ֥ד אֶת־לֵ֖ב מַתָּנָֽה׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὡς φωνὴ ἀκανθῶν ὑπὸ τὸν λέβητα, οὕτως γέλως τῶν ἀφρόνων· καί γε τοῦτο ματαιότης.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Surely extortion maketh the wise man foolish; and a bribe destroyeth the understanding.
					\item[NIV] Extortion turns a wise person into a fool, and a bribe corrupts the heart.
					\item[NRSV] Surely oppression makes the wise foolish, and a bribe corrupts the heart.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:8} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-8
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-8}

			Better is the end of a thing than the beginning thereof: and the patient in spirit is better than the proud in spirit.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֛וֹב אַחֲרִ֥ית דָּבָ֖ר מֵֽרֵאשִׁית֑וֹ ט֥וֹב אֶֽרֶךְ־ר֖וּחַ מִגְּבַהּ־רֽוּחַ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι ἡ συκοφαντία περιφέρει σοφὸν καὶ ἀπόλλυσι τὴν καρδίαν εὐγενείας αὐτοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Better is the end of a thing than the beginning thereof; and the patient in spirit is better than the proud in spirit.
					\item[NIV] The end of a matter is better than its beginning, and patience is better than pride.
					\item[NRSV] Better is the end of a thing than its beginning; the patient in spirit are better than the proud in spirit.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:9} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-9
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-9}

			Be not hasty in thy spirit to be angry: for anger resteth in the bosom of fools.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אַל־תְּבַהֵ֥ל בְּרֽוּחֲךָ֖ לִכְע֑וֹס כִּ֣י כַ֔עַס בְּחֵ֥יק כְּסִילִ֖ים יָנֽוּחַ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὴ ἐσχάτη λόγων ὑπὲρ ἀρχὴν αὐτοῦ, ἀγαθὸν μακρόθυμος ὑπὲρ ὑψηλὸν πνεύματι.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Be not hasty in thy spirit to be angry; for anger resteth in the bosom of fools.
					\item[NIV] Do not be quickly provoked in your spirit, for anger resides in the lap of fools.
					\item[NRSV] Do not be quick to anger, for anger lodges in the bosom of fools.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:10} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-10
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-10}

			Say not thou, What is the cause that the former days were better than these? for thou dost not enquire wisely concerning this.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אַל־תֹּאמַר֙ מֶ֣ה הָיָ֔ה שֶׁ֤הַיָּמִים֙ הָרִ֣אשֹׁנִ֔ים הָי֥וּ טוֹבִ֖ים מֵאֵ֑לֶּה כִּ֛י לֹ֥א מֵחָכְמָ֖ה שָׁאַ֥לְתָּ עַל־זֶֽה׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{μὴ σπεύσῃς ἐν πνεύματί σου τοῦ θυμοῦσθαι, ὅτι θυμὸς ἐν κόλπῳ ἀφρόνων ἀναπαύσεται.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Say not thou, What is the cause that the former days were better than these? for thou dost not inquire wisely concerning this.
					\item[NIV] Do not say, “Why were the old days better than these?” For it is not wise to ask such questions.
					\item[NRSV] Do not say, “Why were the former days better than these?” For it is not from wisdom that you ask this.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:11} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-11
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-11}

			Wisdom is good with an inheritance: and by it there is profit to them that see the sun.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{טוֹבָ֥ה חָכְמָ֖ה עִֽם־נַחֲלָ֑ה וְיֹתֵ֖ר לְרֹאֵ֥י הַשָּֽׁמֶשׁ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{μὴ εἴπῃς Τί ἐγένετο ὅτι αἱ ἡμέραι αἱ πρότεραι ἦσαν ἀγαθαὶ ὑπὲρ ταύτας; ὅτι οὐκ ἐν σοφίᾳ ἐπηρώτησας περὶ τούτου.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Wisdom is as good as an inheritance; yea, more excellent is it for them that see the sun.
					\item[NIV] Wisdom, like an inheritance, is a good thing and benefits those who see the sun.
					\item[NRSV] Wisdom is as good as an inheritance, an advantage to those who see the sun.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:12} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-12
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-12}

			For wisdom is a defence, and money is a defence: but the excellency of knowledge is, that wisdom giveth life to them that have it.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֛י בְּצֵ֥ל הַֽחָכְמָ֖ה בְּצֵ֣ל הַכָּ֑סֶף וְיִתְר֣וֹן דַּ֔עַת הַֽחָכְמָ֖ה תְּחַיֶּ֥ה בְעָלֶֽיהָ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὴ σοφία μετὰ κληρονομίας καὶ περισσεία τοῖς θεωροῦσιν τὸν ἥλιον·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] For wisdom is a defence, even as money is a defence; but the excellency of knowledge is, that wisdom preserveth the life of him that hath it.
					\item[NIV] Wisdom is a shelter as money is a shelter, but the advantage of knowledge is this: Wisdom preserves those who have it.
					\item[NRSV] For the protection of wisdom is like the protection of money, and the advantage of knowledge is that wisdom gives life to the one who possesses it.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:13} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-13
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-13}

			Consider the work of God: for who can make that straight, which he hath made crooked?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{רְאֵ֖ה אֶת־מַעֲשֵׂ֣ה הָאֱלֹהִ֑ים כִּ֣י מִ֤י יוּכַל֙ לְתַקֵּ֔ן אֵ֖ת אֲשֶׁ֥ר עִוְּתֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ὅτι ἐν σκιᾷ αὐτῆς ἡ σοφία ὡς σκιὰ ἀργυρίου, καὶ περισσεία γνώσεως τῆς σοφίας ζωοποιήσει τὸν παρ᾽ αὐτῆς.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Consider the work of God: for who can make that straight, which he hath made crooked?
					\item[NIV] Consider what God has done: Who can straighten what he has made crooked?
					\item[NRSV] Consider the work of God; who can make straight what he has made crooked?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:14} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-14
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-14}

			In the day of prosperity be joyful, but in the day of adversity consider: God also hath set the one over against the other, to the end that man should find nothing after him.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{בְּי֤וֹם טוֹבָה֙ הֱיֵ֣ה בְט֔וֹב וּבְי֥וֹם רָעָ֖ה רְאֵ֑ה גַּ֣ם אֶת־זֶ֤ה לְעֻמַּת־זֶה֙ עָשָׂ֣ה הָֽאֱלֹהִ֔ים עַל־דִּבְרַ֗ת שֶׁלֹּ֨א יִמְצָ֧א הָֽאָדָ֛ם אַחֲרָ֖יו מְאֽוּמָה׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἴδε τὰ ποιήματα τοῦ θεοῦ· ὅτι τίς δυνήσεται κοσμῆσαι ὃν ἂν ὁ θεὸς διαστρέψῃ αὐτόν;}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] In the day of prosperity be joyful, and in the day of adversity consider; yea, God hath made the one side by side with the other, to the end that man should not find out anything that shall be after him.
					\item[NIV] When times are good, be happy; but when times are bad, consider this: God has made the one as well as the other. Therefore, no one can discover anything about their future.
					\item[NRSV] In the day of prosperity be joyful, and in the day of adversity consider; God has made the one as well as the other, so that mortals may not find out anything that will come after them.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:15} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-15
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-15}

			All things have I seen in the days of my vanity: there is a just man that perisheth in his righteousness, and there is a wicked man that prolongeth his life in his wickedness.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אֶת־הַכֹּ֥ל רָאִ֖יתִי בִּימֵ֣י הֶבְלִ֑י יֵ֤שׁ צַדִּיק֙ אֹבֵ֣ד בְּצִדְק֔וֹ וְיֵ֣שׁ רָשָׁ֔ע מַאֲרִ֖יךְ בְּרָעָתֽוֹ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἐν ἡμέρᾳ ἀγαθωσύνης ζῆθι ἐν ἀγαθῷ, καὶ ἴδε ἐν ἡμέρᾳ κακίας. ἴδε καί γε σὺν τούτῳ συμφώνως τοῦτο ἐποίησεν ὁ θεὸς περὶ λαλιᾶς, ἵνα μὴ εὕρῃ ὁ ἄνθρωπος ὀπίσω αὐτοῦ οὐδέν.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] All this have I seen in my days of vanity: there is a righteous man that perisheth in his righteousness, and there is a wicked man that prolongeth his life in his evil-doing.
					\item[NIV] In this meaningless life of mine I have seen both of these: the righteous perishing in their righteousness, and the wicked living long in their wickedness.
					\item[NRSV] In my vain life I have seen everything; there are righteous people who perish in their righteousness, and there are wicked people who prolong their life in their evildoing.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:16} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-16
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-16}

			Be not righteous over much; neither make thyself over wise: why shouldest thou destroy thyself ?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אַל־תְּהִ֤י צַדִּיק֙ הַרְבֵּ֔ה וְאַל־תִּתְחַכַּ֖ם יוֹתֵ֑ר לָ֖מָּה תִּשּׁוֹמֵֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Τὰ πάντα εἶδον ἐν ἡμέραις ματαιότητός μου· ἔστιν δίκαιος ἀπολλύμενος ἐν δικαίῳ αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἔστιν ἀσεβὴς μένων ἐν κακίᾳ αὐτοῦ.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Be not righteous overmuch; neither make thyself overwise: why shouldest thou destroy thyself?
					\item[NIV] Do not be overrighteous, neither be overwise— why destroy yourself?
					\item[NRSV] Do not be too righteous, and do not act too wise; why should you destroy yourself?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:17} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-17
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-17}

			Be not over much wicked, neither be thou foolish: why shouldest thou die before thy time?

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{אַל־תִּרְשַׁ֥ע הַרְבֵּ֖ה וְאַל־תְּהִ֣י סָכָ֑ל לָ֥מָּה תָמ֖וּת בְּלֹ֥א עִתֶּֽךָ׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{μὴ γίνου δίκαιος πολὺ μηδὲ σοφίζου περισσά, μή ποτε ἐκπλαγῇς.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Be not overmuch wicked, neither be thou foolish: why shouldest thou die before thy time?
					\item[NIV] Do not be overwicked, and do not be a fool— why die before your time?
					\item[NRSV] Do not be too wicked, and do not be a fool; why should you die before your time?
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:18} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-18
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-18}

			It is good that thou shouldest take hold of this; yea, also from this withdraw not thine hand: for he that feareth God shall come forth of them all.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{ט֚וֹב אֲשֶׁ֣ר תֶּאֱחֹ֣ז בָּזֶ֔ה וְגַם־מִזֶּ֖ה אַל־תַּנַּ֣ח אֶת־יָדֶ֑ךָ כִּֽי־יְרֵ֥א אֱלֹהִ֖ים יֵצֵ֥א אֶת־כֻּלָּֽם׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{μὴ ἀσεβήσῃς πολὺ καὶ μὴ γίνου σκληρός, ἵνα μὴ ἀποθάνῃς ἐν οὐ καιρῷ σου.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] It is good that thou shouldest take hold of this; yea, also from that withdraw not thy hand: for he that feareth God shall come forth from them all.
					\item[NIV] It is good to grasp the one and not let go of the other. Whoever fears God will avoid all extremes.
					\item[NRSV] It is good that you should take hold of the one, without letting go of the other; for the one who fears God shall succeed with both.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:19} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-19
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-19}

			Wisdom strengtheneth the wise more than ten mighty men which are in the city.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{הַֽחָכְמָ֖ה תָּעֹ֣ז לֶחָכָ֑ם מֵֽעֲשָׂרָה֙ שַׁלִּיטִ֔ים אֲשֶׁ֥ר הָי֖וּ בָּעִֽיר׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἀντέχεσθαί σε ἐν τούτῳ, καί γε ἀπὸ τούτου μὴ μιάνῃς τὴν χεῖρά σου, ὅτι φοβουμένοις τὸν θεὸν ἐξελεύσεται τὰ πάντα.}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Wisdom is a strength to the wise man more than ten rulers that are in a city.
					\item[NIV] Wisdom makes one wise person more powerful than ten rulers in a city.
					\item[NRSV] Wisdom gives strength to the wise more than ten rulers that are in a city.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
			\end{versebox}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Ecclesiastes 7:20} % *ECCLESIASTES-7-20
		% % ---
			\label{ECCLESIASTES-7-20}

			For there is not a just man upon earth, that doeth good, and sinneth not.

			\begin{versebox}
					\item[BHS] \he{כִּ֣י אָדָ֔ם אֵ֥ין צַדִּ֖יק בָּאָ֑רֶץ אֲשֶׁ֥ר יַעֲשֶׂה־טּ֖וֹב וְלֹ֥א יֶחֱטָֽא׃}
					\item[LXX] \gr{Ἡ σοφία βοηθήσει τῷ σοφῷ ὑπὲρ δέκα ἐξουσιάζοντας τοὺς ὄντας ἐν τῇ πόλει·}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					\item[ASV] Surely there is not a righteous man upon earth, that doeth good, and sinneth not.
					\item[NIV] Indeed, there is no one on earth who is righteous, no one who does what is right and never sins.
					\item[NRSV] Surely there is no one on earth so righteous as to do good without ever sinning.
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
